Delusions\n  description: Fixed beliefs that do not change easily, even in the face of contradictory evidence.\n  manifestation: I know it sounds odd, but I keep feeling that certain dates and names in our family papers are being changed or hidden from me, like the hospital or someone in the family is sending me little messages through the records.\n- key: S021\n  name: Disorganized_Thinking_Speech\n  description: Symptoms where speech or thought appears scattered and difficult to follow due to a lack of logical or consistent flow.\n  manifestation: When I try to explain the appointments or the genealogy notes, I start with my sister’s medicine list and then I’m suddenly talking about our grandmother’s birth certificate and the county clerk, and people tell me they can’t follow what I mean.\n- key: S022\n  name: Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior\n  description: A state where behavior and motor control are severely disturbed, appearing as movements or reactions inappropriate to the situation.\n  manifestation: I catch myself lining up papers and pill bottles over and over on the kitchen table, tapping each stack a certain number of times, even when I know I’m supposed to be getting ready to leave.\n- key: S024\n  name: Depressed_Mood\n  description: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.\n  manifestation: Most mornings I wake up with a heavy, low feeling, like the color has gone out of the day before I even get dressed.\n- key: S025\n  name: Loss_of_Interest\n  description: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.\n  manifestation: I used to look forward to watercolor class, quilting circle, and book club, but now I often leave the bag by the door and just can’t make myself care enough to go.\n- key: S028\n  name: Psychomotor_Changes\n  description: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.\n  manifestation: My body feels slowed down lately, like it takes me twice as long to cross the room or button my sweater, though sometimes I pace around the house with the same papers in my hand.\n- key: S031\n  name: Diminished_Thinking\n  description: Diminished ability to think or concentrate, or indecisiveness.\n  manifestation: I sit there staring at simple things, like whether to call the pharmacy or open the mail first, and my mind gets so foggy that I end up doing neither.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: I don’t have a plan to hurt myself, but I do find myself thinking that if I didn’t wake up one morning, it might be a relief to everyone.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: When I am in that low, colorless state, I am also most caught up in the idea that the family papers and hospital notes names in the records were being changed to signal something.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I have stopped teaching my craft It’s like the day already feels heavy before I even get dressed, like there’s no color in it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been going on for quite a while… about a year and two months now. That low, colorless feeling has just kind of stayed with me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're no longer able to enjoy things that normally would bring you pleasure?\n\nPatient: Yes. Those things I used to enjoy, like watercolor class, quilting, and book club… I just don’t get much pleasure from them now. Sometimes I even get my bag ready and leave it by the door, but then I don’t go. I just can’t make myself care enough.\n\nDoctor: Have your feelings of sadness or low mood been consistently the same over the past year, or have they varied in intensity?\n\nPatient: It varies some, but most mornings it’s there. I wake up with that heavy, low feeling, like the color’s gone out of the day before I even get dressed. Some days it’s a little lighter, I guess, but it hasn’t really gone away.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods where you felt completely back to normal between episodes of feeling low?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I might have a day here or there that feels a little easier, but I haven’t felt completely back to normal. This low, colorless stretch has been with me for about a year and two months.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods, even brief ones, where you felt completely better and then the low feelings returned?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. There might be a short time where it feels not quite as bad, but I haven’t felt completely better. It’s been going on, that low, colorless feeling, for about a year and two months now.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice any kind of pattern to when your low feelings are worse or better?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly worse in the mornings. I wake up and it’s already there, that heavy low feeling, like the day has lost its color before I even get dressed. It may ease a little later sometimes, but mornings are the main pattern I notice.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your low feelings are related to the time of year in any way?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really noticed it changing with the seasons. It’s more that most mornings I wake up with that heavy, low feeling, like the color’s already gone out of the day before I even get dressed.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any period, no matter how brief, where your low mood significantly improved before returning?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. It might ease a little now and then, but most mornings I still wake up with that heavy low feeling, like the day has already lost its color before I even get dressed.\n\nDoctor: Have you had at least two months where you felt completely normal and without low mood at any point during the past year and two months?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’ve had two whole months like that. This low, colorless feeling has been going on for about a year and two months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time, say a few weeks or more, where you felt consistently low, followed by a period where that low feeling lifted, and then returned again?\n\nPatient: No, not really in a clear up-and-down way. It’s more just there most mornings when I wake up—this heavy, low feeling, like the day has already lost its color before I even get dressed.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a clear period where you felt completely low, and then a separate period where you felt completely better, before feeling low again?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say there’s been a clear separate stretch where I felt all better. It’s more that most mornings I wake up with that heavy, low feeling, like the color’s gone out of the day before I even get dressed.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were completely over this low feeling for a while, only to have it come back later?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don’t feel like I’ve been completely over it. Most mornings I still wake up with that heavy, low feeling, like the day’s already lost its color before I’m even dressed.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt unusually happy or elevated for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I can’t recall feeling unusually happy or high for several days like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were completely back to your normal self for at least a couple of weeks at any point over the last year?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’ve been completely back to my normal self for a couple of weeks. This whole change has been going on about a year and five months now, since my sister’s health took that serious turn.\n\nDoctor: Have you had at least two weeks where you felt like your old self again?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I have. Like I said, this change has been going on for about a year and five months now, since my sister’s health got so bad. I haven’t really had two weeks where I felt like my old self again.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
